The company operates three independent EHR platforms — **CareLogic** (enterprise/multi-site), **Credible** (large enterprise, #1 in KLAS for Behavioral Health), and **InSync** (small to mid-size practices). Qualifacts was acquired by Warburg Pincus in September 2019. Qualifacts and Credible Behavioral Health merged in August 2020. InSync Healthcare Solutions was acquired by the combined entity in December 2021, which then rebranded simply as \"Qualifacts.\" The old InSync website (insynchcs.com) now redirects to qualifacts.com.\n     9→\n    10→The combined company serves over 2,500 customers representing 75,000 providers and more than 10 million patients across all 50 states. The InSync acquisition reportedly doubled the organization's customer count. InSync was originally an independent company providing EHR and practice management software plus revenue cycle management services, targeting smaller behavioral health, medical, and rehabilitative practices — a market segment where approximately 40% of behavioral health providers work in organizations with fewer than 50 full-time employees.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Insync EMR/PM\n    13→\n    14→CHPL ID: 10858\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→InSync EMR/PM is a cloud-based, fully integrated electronic health record and practice management platform. The product name (\"EMR/PM\") reflects that it combines clinical EHR functionality with practice management (scheduling, billing, revenue cycle) in a single system. It is hosted on Amazon Web Services (AWS) with redundant Level 1 data warehouse servers and claims 99% uptime. The platform uses Model-View-Controller (MVC) technology for cross-device browser-based access.\n    19→\n    20→The certified module encompasses the full product — InSync EMR/PM is not a component of a larger suite but rather is itself the complete integrated platform. The CHPL certification covers version 10 and includes a broad set of criteria: clinical data (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); patient portal (e)(1); public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(2), (f)(7); and FHIR APIs (g)(10). The SED intended user description is \"Medical/Clinical Providers.\"\n    21→\n    22→### Users & Market\n    23→\n    24→InSync targets **small to mid-size behavioral health, rehabilitative therapy, and medical practices**. Qualifacts markets it to Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs) as well. A case study references a Michigan mental health practice (KindMind) that scaled using InSync's mental health EMR capabilities.\n    33→\n    34→G2 rating: 4.2/5 based on 45 reviews. management services (also offered as a separate managed service)\n    93→- Links between scheduling, clinical, and billing modules to reduce duplicate work\n    94→\n    95→**Telehealth**\n    96→- support\n   125→- HIPAA compliance\n   126→\n   127→### Data & Content\n   128→\n   129→Based on the features described above, InSync stores and manages the following categories of data:\n   130→\n   131→**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, medical/behavioral health history, clinical encounter notes (therapy notes, progress notes, treatment plans, group notes), assessments (including ASAM, evidence-based behavioral health instruments), vitals, diagnoses (ICD-10), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, immunization records (implied by public health reporting criteria (f)(1)-(f)(2)), lab orders and results, referral records.\n   132→\n   133→**Medication data**: ePrescription records, medication administration records (eMAR), controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), PDMP query data, drug interaction check logs, prescription history, refill records.\n   134→\n   135→**Scheduling & appointment data**: Appointment schedules across providers and rooms, appointment reminders (text/phone/email), no-show tracking, telehealth session records.\n   136→\n   137→**Billing & financial data**: Claims data, ICD codes linked to encounters, insurance information, payment records, account balances, receipts, revenue cycle data. The billing module's rules engine and automated code application mean the system stores detailed claim lifecycle data.\n   138→\n   139→**Patient portal data**: Secure messages between patients and providers, patient-completed intake forms and assessments, eSignatures, payment transactions, portal activity logs, broadcast messages.\n   140→\n   141→**Telehealth data**: Video session records/metadata, in-session documentation, group therapy session data.\n   142→\n   143→**Documents & forms**: Custom dynamic forms, uploaded documents, scanned documents (OCR), e-faxes, eSignatures.\n   144→\n   145→**Administrative data**: User/provider profiles, role-based access configurations, task assignments, notification/alert logs, audit trails (implied by certification criteria (d)(1)-(d)(9)), system configuration and workflow customization settings.\n   146→\n   147→**Reporting data**: Built-in report outputs spanning clinical, financial, utilization, productivity, and referral analytics.\n   148→\n   149→**Public health reporting data**: Immunization data for registries (f)(1), syndromic surveillance data (f)(2), and clinical data registry reporting (f)(7) — all required by certification.\n   150→\n   151→The vendor website does not discuss imaging storage, radiology, or surgical documentation — consistent with InSync's focus on behavioral health, rehabilitative therapy, and ambulatory medical practices rather than hospital or surgical settings. The company operates three independent EHR platforms — **CareLogic** (enterprise/multi-site), **Credible** (large enterprise, #1 in KLAS for Behavioral Health), and **InSync** (small to mid-size practices). Qualifacts was acquired by Warburg Pincus in September 2019. Qualifacts and Credible Behavioral Health merged in August 2020. InSync Healthcare Solutions was acquired by the combined entity in December 2021, which then rebranded simply as \"Qualifacts.\" The old InSync website (insynchcs.com) now redirects to qualifacts.com.\n\nThe combined company serves over 2,500 customers representing 75,000 providers and more than 10 million patients across all 50 states. The InSync acquisition reportedly doubled the organization's customer count. InSync was originally an independent company providing EHR and practice management software plus revenue cycle management services, targeting smaller behavioral health, medical, and rehabilitative practices — a market segment where approximately 40% of behavioral health providers work in organizations with fewer than 50 full-time employees.\n\n## Product: Insync EMR/PM\n\nCHPL ID: 10858\n\n### What It Is\n\nInSync EMR/PM is a cloud-based, fully integrated electronic health record and practice management platform. The product name (\"EMR/PM\") reflects that it combines clinical EHR functionality with practice management (scheduling, billing, revenue cycle) in a single system. It is hosted on Amazon Web Services (AWS) with redundant Level 1 data warehouse servers and claims 99% uptime. The platform uses Model-View-Controller (MVC) technology for cross-device browser-based access.\n\nThe certified module encompasses the full product — InSync EMR/PM is not a component of a larger suite but rather is itself the complete integrated platform. The CHPL certification covers version 10 and includes a broad set of criteria: clinical data (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14)-(a)(15); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); patient portal (e)(1); public health reporting (f)(1)-(f)(2), (f)(7); and FHIR APIs (g)(10). The SED intended user description is \"Medical/Clinical Providers.\"\n\n### Users & Market\n\nInSync targets **small to mid-size behavioral health, rehabilitative therapy, and medical practices**. Core user types include:\n\n- **Behavioral health providers**: Mental health therapists, psychiatrists, substance use disorder (SUD) counselors, addiction treatment professionals\n- **Rehabilitative therapy**: Physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech-language pathologists\n- **Medical/primary care**: Family medicine, internal medicine, pediatrics, OB/GYN, orthopedics, occupational medicine, cardiology, dermatology, and other specialties\n- **IDD providers**: Intellectual and developmental disability service organizations\n- **Practice staff**: Billing staff, front desk/schedulers, practice managers, administrators\n\nThe platform serves solo practitioners, small group practices, and mid-size agencies. Qualifacts markets it to Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs) as well. A case study references a Michigan mental health practice (KindMind) that scaled using InSync's mental health EMR capabilities.\n\nG2 rating: 4.2/5 based on 45 reviews. TechRadar gave it 2/5, though that review focused more on the vendor's website transparency than on product capabilities.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nBased on vendor materials, product pages, and third-party review sites, InSync includes the following integrated modules and features:\n\n**Clinical Documentation & Charting**\n- AI-powered clinical documentation (Qualifacts iQ) that converts session transcripts into compliant clinical notes (DAP, BIRP, Progress Notes, Treatment Plans)\n- Therapy progress notes with specialized templates for individual therapy, group therapy, and telehealth sessions\n- Treatment planning with progress/outcomes tracking\n- Clinical workflow customization with dynamic forms (drag-and-drop form builder)\n- Customizable macros, frequently used notes, and personalized workflows\n- Voice recognition support\n- EM coding tools\n\n**Assessment & Measurement-Based Care**\n- Library of evidence-based assessments\n- Pre-appointment assessments (completed via patient portal)\n- ASAM criteria support (for addiction/SUD severity assessment)\n- Measurement-based care tracking\n- Mental health assessment tools\n\n**ePrescribing & Medication Management**\n- Integrated ePrescribing with EPCS (Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances)\n- eMAR (electronic medication administration records)\n- Drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction/contraindication checks\n- PDMP (Prescription Drug Monitoring Program) integration\n- Refill management\n- Medication history viewing\n- Pharmacy search and interface\n- Eligibility checking for prescriptions\n\n**Scheduling & Patient Intake**\n- Multi-provider, multi-room scheduling view\n- Appointment reminders via text, phone, and email\n- Patient self-scheduling via portal\n- Telehealth appointment scheduling\n- API-based patient intake and website registration\n- Digital intake forms with eSignature\n- Check-in kiosk support (tablet-based)\n\n**Patient/Client Portal**\n- HIPAA-compliant secure messaging between patients and providers\n- Appointment requests and scheduling\n- Pre-visit assessment and intake form completion\n- View treatment plans\n- View and pay account balances (with real-time balance updates and receipts)\n- Access to clinical and financial information\n- Telehealth session access from portal\n- Broadcast messaging for mass client communications\n- Branded portal with organizational customization\n- Mobile-friendly\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle Management**\n- Integrated billing engine with automated claims management\n- Rules engine that auto-applies ICD codes to appointments and carries them through the billing workflow\n- Claims queue management and submission\n- Insurance/claims processing\n- Revenue cycle management services (also offered as a separate managed service)\n- Links between scheduling, clinical, and billing modules to reduce duplicate work\n\n**Telehealth**\n- Integrated video telehealth (no separate system login required)\n- Group video sessions (up to 50 participants)\n- Telepsychiatry support\n- Documentation during live sessions\n- ePrescribing from telehealth sessions\n\n**Lab Integration & Referrals**\n- Lab interfacing (listed as an add-on)\n- Lab orders and results\n- Referral management\n- E-faxing (add-on)\n\n**Reporting & Analytics**\n- 100+ built-in reports covering patient appointments, financial summaries, utilization, lab results, productivity, referrals\n- Healthcare analytics and reporting dashboards\n- Customizable dashboards with role-based access\n- Data-driven decision support\n\n**Administrative & Other**\n- Patient demographics management\n- Medical history tracking\n- Document management\n- Task management\n- OCR card scanning (add-on)\n- eSignature pad support (add-on)\n- Notifications and alerts for care teams\n- Caseload management tools\n- HL7 interoperability support\n- ICD-10 coding support\n- HIPAA compliance\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the features described above, InSync stores and manages the following categories of data:\n\n**Clinical data**: Patient demographics, medical/behavioral health history, clinical encounter notes (therapy notes, progress notes, treatment plans, group notes), assessments (including ASAM, evidence-based behavioral health instruments), vitals, diagnoses (ICD-10), problem lists, medication lists, allergy lists, immunization records (implied by public health reporting criteria (f)(1)-(f)(2)), lab orders and results, referral records.\n\n**Medication data**: ePrescription records, medication administration records (eMAR), controlled substance prescriptions (EPCS), PDMP query data, drug interaction check logs, prescription history, refill records.\n\n**Scheduling & appointment data**: Appointment schedules across providers and rooms, appointment reminders (text/phone/email), no-show tracking, telehealth session records.\n\n**Billing & financial data**: Claims data, ICD codes linked to encounters, insurance information, payment records, account balances, receipts, revenue cycle data. 10</p>\n    47→<section id=\"single-patient-export\">\n    48→<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n    49→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    50→<div class=\"line\">Patients and their authorized representatives must request the Single Patient export from their provider directly.</div>\n    51→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    52→<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff initiate the Single Patient Export process in the InSync application.</div>\n    53→<div class=\"line\">Upon completion, a notification is sent with a link to download the Single Patient Export file.</div>\n    54→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    55→<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff cannot provide Single Patient EHI Exports on behalf of any agency.</div>\n    56→</div>\n    57→</section>\n    58→<section id=\"patient-population-export\">\n    59→<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n    60→<div class=\"line-block\">\n    61→<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff request the Patient Population Export by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center.</div>\n    62→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    63→<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff will initiate the Patient Population Export process.</div>\n    64→<div class=\"line\">Upon completion, a notification is sent with a link to download the Patient Population Export file.</div>\n    65→<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n    66→<div class=\"line\">Please allow up to 30 calendar days for the export process to complete.</div>\n    67→</div>\n    68→<div class=\"admonition note\">\n    69→<p id=\"single-patient-export\">\n<h2>Single Patient Export</h2>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">Patients and their authorized representatives must request the Single Patient export from their provider directly.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff initiate the Single Patient Export process in the InSync application.</div>\n<div class=\"line\">Upon completion, a notification is sent with a link to download the Single Patient Export file.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff cannot provide Single Patient EHI Exports on behalf of any agency.</div>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"patient-population-export\">\n<h2>Patient Population Export</h2>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">Designated agency staff request the Patient Population Export by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Qualifacts staff will initiate the Patient Population Export process.</div>\n<div class=\"line\">Upon completion, a notification is sent with a link to download the Patient Population Export file.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">Please allow up to 30 calendar days for the export process to complete.</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"admonition note\">\n<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n<p>Only one Patient Population Export can be requested at a time.\nBy requesting a Patient Population Export, you agree to accept and be fully bound by the <a class=\"reference external\" href=\"https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/_downloads/Qualifacts_EHI_Export_Terms_of_Use_SEP_2024.pdf\">Qualifacts Terms and Conditions</a>.</p>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"accessing-the-ehi-export\">\n<h2>Accessing the EHI Export</h2>\n<p>Designated agency staff access the completed Single Patient and Patient Population EHI exports directly in InSync through the requester’s To Do list.</p>\n<div class=\"admonition note\">\n<p class=\"admonition-title\">Note</p>\n<p>All EHI Export download links automatically expire after 30 calendar days.</p>\n</div>\n</section>\n<section id=\"technical-documentation\">\n<h2>Technical Documentation</h2>\n<section id=\"file-types\">\n<h3>File Types</h3>\n<div class=\"line-block\">\n<div class=\"line\">The Single Patient Export consists of a zip file containing a Newline Delimited JSON file and a folder, “Supported File”, for any attachments from the patient’s record.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">The Patient Population Export zip contains one JSON file per patient.  All attachments for all patients are contained in the “Supported File” folder.</div>\n<div class=\"line\"><br /></div>\n<div class=\"line\">The attachments in the “Supported File” folder are linked to the corresponding patient through the “Documents / Images” array using the DownloadedFilePath:</div>\n</div>\n<div class=\"highlight-default notranslate\"><div class=\"highlight\"><pre><span></span><span class=\"s2\">&quot;Documents / Images&quot;</span><span class=\"p\">:</span> <span class=\"p\">[</span>\n        <span class=\"p\">{</span>\n scriptDir = dirname(new URL(import.meta.url).pathname);\nconst xlsPath = resolve(scriptDir, \"../InSync_EHI_Export_Data_Dictionary.xls\");\nconst wb = XLSX.readFile(xlsPath);\n\nconst SKIP_SHEETS = new Set([\"Confidential Notice\", \"INDEX\", \"Changelogs\"]);\n\ninterface Field {\n  row_num: number;\n  column_name: string;\n  data_type: string;\n  nullable: boolean;\n  description: string;\n  remarks: string;\n}\n\ninterface Section {\n  sheet_name: string;\n  section_name: string;\n  description: string;\n  category: string;\n  module: string;\n  fields: Field[];\n}\n\ninterface IndexEntry {\n  row_num: number;\n  section_name: string;\n  category: string;\n  module: string;\n  details: string;\n  remarks: string;\n}\n\n// Parse the INDEX sheet for category/module metadata\nconst indexSheet = wb.Sheets[\"INDEX\"];\nconst indexData = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(indexSheet, {\n  header: 1,\n  defval: \"\",\n}) as string[][];\n\nconst indexEntries: IndexEntry[] = [];\nfor (let i = 3; i < indexData.length; i++) {\n  const row = indexData[i];\n  if (!row[1]) continue;\n  indexEntries.push({\n    row_num: Number(row[0]) || i - 2,\n    section_name: String(row[1]).trim(),\n    category: String(row[2] || \"\").trim(),\n    module: String(row[3] || \"\").trim(),\n    details: String(row[4] || \"\").trim(),\n    remarks: String(row[5] || \"\").trim(),\n  });\n}\n\n// Build a lookup from sheet name prefix to index entry\nfunction findIndexEntry(sheetName: string): IndexEntry | undefined {\n  // Try exact match first, then prefix match (sheets are truncated at 31 chars)\n  return (\n    indexEntries.find(\n      (e) => e.section_name.toLowerCase() === sheetName.toLowerCase()\n    ) ||\n    indexEntries.find((e) =>\n      e.section_name.toLowerCase().startsWith(sheetName.toLowerCase())\n    ) ||\n    indexEntries.find((e) =>\n      sheetName.toLowerCase().startsWith(e.section_name.toLowerCase().slice(0, 30))\n    )\n  );\n}\n\n// Parse the Changelogs sheet\nconst changelogSheet = wb.Sheets[\"Changelogs\"];\nconst changelogData = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(changelogSheet, {\n  header: 1,\n  defval: \"\",\n}) as (!isNaN(Number(row[1]))) {\n    const d = XLSX.SSF.parse_date_code(Number(row[1]));\n    dateStr = `${d.y}-${String(d.m).padStart(2, \"0\")}-${String(d.d).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n  }\n Parse each data sheet\nconst sections: Section[] = [];\nconst parseFailures: { sheet_name: string; error: string }[] = [];\n\nfor (const sheetName of wb.SheetNames) {\n  if (SKIP_SHEETS.has(sheetName)) continue;\n\n  try {\n    const ws = wb.Sheets[sheetName];\n    const data = XLSX.utils.sheet_to_json(ws, {\n      header: 1,\n      defval: \"\",\n    }) as string[][];\n\n    // Find the description (row 1, col 2)\n    const description = String(data[1]?.[2] || \"\").trim();\n\n    // Find the header row (contains \"Name of the Column\")\n    let headerRowIdx = -1;\n    for (let i = 0; i < Math.min(10, data.length); i++) {\n      const row = data[i].map((c) => String(c).trim().toLowerCase());\n      if (row.includes(\"name of the column\")) {\n        headerRowIdx = i;\n        break;\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (headerRowIdx === -1) {\n      parseFailures.push({\n        sheet_name: sheetName,\n        error: \"Could not find header row with 'Name of the Column'\",\n      });\n      continue;\n    }\n\n    // Extract fields\n    const fields: Field[] = [];\n    for (let i = headerRowIdx + 1; i < data.length; i++) {\n      const row = data[i];\n      const colName = String(row[1] || \"\").trim();\n      if (!colName) continue; // skip empty rows\n\n      fields.push({\n        row_num: Number(row[0]) || fields.length + 1,\n        column_name: colName,\n        data_type: String(row[2] || \"\").trim(),\n        nullable: String(row[3] || \"\")\n          .trim()\n          .toLowerCase() === \"yes\",\n        description: String(row[4] || \"\").trim(),\n        remarks: String(row[5] || \"\").trim(),\n      });\n    }\n\n    const indexEntry = findIndexEntry(sheetName);\n\n    sections.push({\n      sheet_name: sheetName,\n      section_name: indexEntry?.section_name || sheetName,\n      description,\n      category: indexEntry?.category || \"\",\n      module: indexEntry?.module || \"\",\n      fields,\n    });\n  } catch (e: any) {\n    parseFailures.push({\n      sheet_name: sheetName,\n      error: e.message || String(e),\n    });\n  }\n}\n\n// Build output\nconst output = {\n  source: \"InSync_EHI_Export_Data_Dictionary.xls\",\n  extracted_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n  last_updated: \"November 2025\",\n  changelogs,\n  total_sections: sections.length,\n  total_fields: sections.reduce((sum, s) => sum + s.fields.length, 0),\n  sections,\n};\n\nconst stats = {\n  total_sheets: wb.SheetNames.length,\n  skipped_sheets: Array.from(SKIP_SHEETS),\n  sheets_parsed: sections.length,\n  sheets_failed: parseFailures.length,\n  parse_failures: parseFailures,\n  total_fields: output.total_fields,\n  sections_summary: sections.map((s) => ({\n    section_name: s.section_name,\n    sheet_name: s.sheet_name,\n    category: s.category,\n    module: s.module,\n    field_count: s.fields.length,\n  })),\n};\n\nwriteFileSync(\n  resolve(scriptDir, \"data-dictionary.json\"),\n  JSON.stringify(output, null, 2)\n);\nwriteFileSync(\n  resolve(scriptDir, \"extraction-stats.json\"),\n  JSON.stringify(stats, null, 2)\n);\n\nconsole.log(`Sections parsed: ${sections.length}`);\nconsole.log(`Total fields: ${output.total_fields}`);\nconsole.log(`Parse failures: ${parseFailures.length}`);\nif (parseFailures.length > 0) {\n  for (const f of Script parses all 84 sheets (81 data sections after skipping 3 metadata sheets), extracts 960 fields with full metadata. Zero parse failures. Output: `data-dictionary.json` (241 KB) and `extraction-stats.json` (15 KB).\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nInSync provides two EHI export modes:\n\n1. **Single Patient Export**: Initiated by designated agency staff within the InSync application upon patient/authorized representative request. Produces a ZIP file containing a Newline Delimited JSON file and a \"Supported File\" folder for attachments.\n\n2. **Patient Population Export**: Requested by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center. Qualifacts staff initiates the process. Produces a ZIP containing one JSON file per patient, with all attachments in a shared \"Supported File\" folder. May take up to 30 calendar days to complete. Only one can be requested at a time, and requesting requires acceptance of the Qualifacts EHI Export Terms of Use.\n\nBoth exports are accessed through the requester's To Do list within InSync. Download links expire after 30 calendar days.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export format is **Newline Delimited JSON** (NDJSON) — a vendor-defined flat JSON structure, not FHIR. This is a genuine (b)(10) export format, not a repackaging of the (g)(10) FHIR API. The documentation explicitly separates EHI export from FHIR API access (the parent page links to their FHIR API documentation separately).\n\nAttachments (documents, images) are exported as physical files in a \"Supported File\" folder, linked to the patient JSON via the `DownloadedFilePath` field in the \"Documents / Images\" array. The page includes a JSON example showing this structure:\n\n```json\n\"Documents / Images\": [\n    {\n        \"FileDescription\": \"Patient's Insurance Card\",\n        \"FolderName\": \"Insurance Card\",\n        \"FileType\": \"jpg\",\n        \"FileCreationDate\": \"2023-09-06T05:21:39.97\",\n        \"DownloadedFilePath\": \"~/Supported File/12345_Front_98765.jpg\"\n    }\n]\n```\n\n### Data Dictionary\n\nThe data dictionary is the primary technical documentation artifact. It is an XLS file with 84 sheets:\n\n- **3 metadata sheets**: Confidential Notice, INDEX (table of contents with category/module classification), Changelogs (7 versions from initial release through Sprint 69/USCDI v3.1, last updated November 2025)\n- **81 data section sheets**, each documenting the fields exported for a specific data domain\n\nEach data section sheet contains:\n- A description of the section\n- A field-level table with columns: Row #, Name of the Column, Data Type, Nullable, Used For (description), Remarks\n\nThe 81 sections cover **960 total fields**. Field data types include String, INT, Date, DateTime, and Boolean. The \"Used For\" column provides brief English descriptions. The \"Remarks\" column is sparsely populated.\n\n### Changelog History\n\n| Version | Date | Description |\n|---------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | 2023-09-13 | Initial Release |\n| 2 | 2023-10-11 | Second release with new sections |\n| 3 | 2023-10-15 | Third release with new sections |\n| 4 | 2024-07-30 | Sprint 36 — new fields added |\n| 5 | 2025-01-24 | Sprint 49 — Fractional Units in Patient Program |\n| 6 | 2025-06-27 | Sprint 60 — Out-of-Pocket Payments in Collection Information |\n| 7 | 2025-10-31 | Sprint 69 — changes related to USCDI v3.1 |\n\nThe dictionary file metadata confirms last save date of 2025-11-17.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe InSync EHI export data dictionary demonstrates **remarkably broad coverage** across clinical and billing domains, which is notable for a (b)(10) compliance effort. Here is a domain-by-domain assessment, referencing the product research:\n\n**Well-covered clinical domains:**\n- **Patient demographics** (64 fields) — comprehensive, including SSN, race, ethnicity, preferred language, communication preferences, emergency contacts\n- **Allergies** (11 fields) — includes RXCUI, SNOMED codes, severity, reactions\n- **Problems/Diagnoses** (8 fields) — ICD codes, onset/resolved dates\n- **Medications** (12 fields) — includes RXCUI, prescribing details, sig instructions\n- **Immunizations** (18 fields) — CVX codes, manufacturer, lot numbers\n- **Vital signs** (43 fields) — very detailed, including BMI, blood pressure, pulse ox, pain scale\n- **Clinical notes** (13 fields) — encounter notes with provider, date, type\n- **Lab results / Clinical tests** (12 fields) — LOINC codes, values, units, reference ranges\n- **Procedures** (10 fields) — CPT/HCPCS codes, modifiers\n- **Goals** (11 fields) — treatment goals with target dates and status\n- **Care team members** (8 fields) — providers, therapists\n- **Referrals** (6 fields) — referral tracking\n- **Family health history** (15 fields) — conditions by family member\n- **Medical devices** (14 fields) — implant details with UDI\n\n**Well-covered billing/financial domains:**\n- **Claims** (18 fields) — claim numbers, payers, submission dates, transmission types\n- **Billing codes assigned** (3 fields) — CPT code linkage\n- **Billing statements and summaries** (6 fields)\n- **Charges, refunds, deductibles** (6 fields) — payment details\n- **Collection information** (3 fields) — copay collection\n- **Denials** (4 fields) — claim denials\n- **Explanation of benefits** (8 fields) — ERA/payer remittance\n- **Health insurance information** (46 fields) — very detailed payer coverage\n- **Eligibility information** (6 fields)\n- **Prior authorizations** (21 fields) — authorization tracking\n- **Price estimates** (17 fields) — patient cost estimates\n- **Financial assistance** (16 fields) — sliding fee scale\n- **List of prices/charges** (2 fields)\n\n**Specialty and behavioral health domains:**\n- **Psychiatric history** (7 fields) — directly relevant to InSync's behavioral health focus\n- **Behavioral data** (4 fields)\n- **Substance abuse** (5 fields) — consumption details\n- **Assessments and treatment plans** (7 fields) — treatment planning data\n- **Social history** (13 fields for consumption, 2 for elements)\n- **Smoking status** (8 fields)\n\n**OB/GYN and specialty clinical data:**\n- **Pregnancy information** (13 fields)\n- **Newborn delivery** (26 fields)\n- **Gynecological history** (18 fields)\n- **Past pregnancy data** (19 fields)\n- **Medical history (Ob/Gyn)** (13 fields)\n- **Pregnancy intent** (2 fields), **Pregnancy status** (5 fields)\n\n**Additional domains:**\n- **Documents/images** (5 fields) — with physical file export\n- **Additional/clinical forms** (4 fields) — custom forms\n- **Custom fields** (2 fields) — demographic custom fields\n- **Consents** (6 fields) — TPO, HIE, medication consents\n- **Encounter information** (21 fields)\n- **Patient relationships** (22 fields) — delegates, emergency contacts\n- **Patient education** (6 fields)\n- **Health concerns/reason for visit** (9 fields)\n- **Functional status** (8 fields)\n- **Patient summary and plan** (21 fields)\n- **Diagnostic imaging** (20 fields)\n- **Care setting histories**: Inpatient Hospice (7), Ambulatory Hospice (6), Palliative Care (6), Long Term Care (5)\n- **Work information** (17 fields)\n- **Surgical history** (9 fields)\n- **Genetic history** (12 fields)\n- **Medical history** (7 fields)\n- **Nutrition and diet** (6 fields)\n- **ROS** (8 fields), **Physical Exam** (7 fields), **Chief Complaints/HPI** (11 fields)\n- **Health maintenance** (6 fields)\n- **Referring diagnosis** (6 fields)\n- **Patient pharmacy** (15 fields)\n- **Program** (29 fields), **Case management** (40 fields)\n- **Census data** (10 fields)\n- **Organization** (20 fields), **Facility data** (8 fields), **Provider** (9 fields)\n- **Credentialing records** (8 fields)\n- **HL7 ADT notifications** (2 fields)\n- **Provenance** (3 fields) — EHI export request tracking\n- **Recorded sex or gender** (4 fields)\n- **Review of results** (7 fields), **Special alerts for care handoffs** (10 fields)\n- **Event logs** (12 fields) — audit trail (note: this is included in the export even though audit logs are not strictly EHI)\n\n**Potentially missing or underrepresented domains:**\n\nBased on the product research, InSync stores data for several features that are not clearly represented in separate data dictionary sections:\n\n- **ePrescribing / EPCS data**: Medication prescriptions are in the Medications section, but specific ePrescribing transaction details (PDMP queries, controlled substance prescription records, pharmacy transmission status) are not explicitly broken out. Some of this may be captured in the Medications fields.\n- **eMAR (electronic medication administration records)**: Not explicitly called out as a separate section, though it may be included within Medications or Clinical notes.\n- **Telehealth session metadata**: No dedicated section for telehealth session records (video session details, session duration, participant info). These may be captured as encounter subtypes.\n- **Secure messaging**: Patient portal secure messages are not explicitly listed as an exported data domain.\n- **Patient portal activity**: Portal-specific data (login history, form submissions via portal) is not represented.\n- **HL7 interface data**: Only \"HL7 ADT notifications\" (2 fields) is present — this is a narrow view of the interface capabilities.\n\nThese gaps are relatively minor and characteristic of a product that exports from its database rather than cherry-picking USCDI elements. Most of these data types may be subsumed within the broad sections (e.g., telehealth encounters within Encounter information, secure messages within Clinical notes or Documents).\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **vendor-defined flat JSON format** — not FHIR, C-CDA, or any recognized standard. This is appropriate for a (b)(10) export because:\n\n1. The format is clearly designed to export the full breadth of InSync's data model, not just USCDI elements.\n2. The field names are descriptive and self-documenting (e.g., `PatientFirstName`, `ClaimNumber`, `AllergiesRXCUI`).\n3. The data dictionary provides adequate field-level documentation for a developer to parse the export.\n4. Clinical codes (ICD, CPT, LOINC, RXCUI, SNOMED, CVX) are preserved in their standard coded forms.\n\nThe format is NOT standardized interoperability-ready (no FHIR, no C-CDA), which means a receiving system would need to build a custom import. However, this is expected for (b)(10) and is arguably more complete than a FHIR-only export would be.\n\nThere is no JSON Schema, OpenAPI spec, or other machine-readable format specification beyond the XLS data dictionary itself.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The data dictionary is extensive (81 sections, 960 fields) and covers both EMR and PM modules.\n- Each field has a name, data type, nullable flag, and brief description.\n- The INDEX sheet provides category/module classification for each section.\n- The changelog shows active maintenance (7 versions over 2+ years, most recently November 2025 with USCDI v3.1 updates).\n- The export page clearly separates single-patient vs. population export workflows.\n- The JSON example for document/attachment linking is helpful.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample export file** — no example JSON showing a complete patient record. Only one JSON snippet for the Documents/Images array.\n- **No JSON schema** — the data dictionary is in XLS format only. No machine-readable schema (JSON Schema, XSD, etc.).\n- **Field descriptions are minimal** — the \"Used For\" column often just restates the column name (e.g., `PatientFirstName` → \"Patient First Name\").\n- **No value sets documented** — coded fields (e.g., `AllergiesSeverityCode`, `PatientGender`, `TransmissionType`) do not specify the allowed values or code systems.\n- **No relationship documentation** — the export appears to be a flat JSON structure per patient, but how the 81 sections relate to each other (e.g., which encounters link to which claims) is not specified.\n- **No cardinality information** — which sections are arrays vs. single values is not documented (though the JSON example implies arrays).\n- **The \"Remarks\" column is almost entirely empty** across all 960 fields.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe field-level documentation is at a **moderate** level of granularity:\n- Field names: yes\n- Data types: yes (String, INT, Date, DateTime, Boolean)\n- Nullable: yes\n- Brief descriptions: yes (though often tautological)\n- Value sets/enumerations: no\n- Relationships/foreign keys: no\n- Cardinality: no\n- Examples: no (except one JSON snippet)\n\nA developer receiving an InSync EHI export could identify and parse the fields but would need to reverse-engineer the structure, relationships, and coded values from actual export data.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nThis is a **genuinely strong (b)(10) implementation** that exports across the full breadth of InSync's clinical and billing data model using a vendor-native JSON format. It is clearly not a repackaging of the (g)(10) FHIR API — the export covers 81 distinct data domains including billing, insurance, financial assistance, case management, and specialty-specific clinical data that would not appear in a USCDI/US Core export.\n\nThe primary limitation is **documentation quality rather than export scope**. The data dictionary covers the right breadth of data but lacks the depth (value sets, relationships, examples, machine-readable schema) that would make it fully self-describing. A developer working with this export would need access to sample data to fill in the gaps.\n\nThe export is actively maintained (last updated November 2025 with USCDI v3.1 changes) and has evolved through 7 versions since its September 2023 initial release. Output: `data-dictionary.json` (241 KB) and `extraction-stats.json` (15 KB).\n\n## What Was Found\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nInSync provides two EHI export modes:\n\n1. **Single Patient Export**: Initiated by designated agency staff within the InSync application upon patient/authorized representative request. Produces a ZIP file containing a Newline Delimited JSON file and a \"Supported File\" folder for attachments.\n\n2. **Patient Population Export**: Requested by submitting a ticket through the Qualifacts Care Center. Qualifacts staff initiates the process. Produces a ZIP containing one JSON file per patient, with all attachments in a shared \"Supported File\" folder. May take up to 30 calendar days to complete. Only one can be requested at a time, and requesting requires acceptance of the Qualifacts EHI Export Terms of Use.\n\nBoth exports are accessed through the requester's To Do list within InSync. Download links expire after 30 calendar days.\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export format is **Newline Delimited JSON** (NDJSON) — a vendor-defined flat JSON structure, not FHIR. This is a genuine (b)(10) export format, not a repackaging of the (g)(10) FHIR API. The documentation explicitly separates EHI export from FHIR API access (the parent page links to their FHIR API documentation separately).\n\nAttachments (documents, images) are exported as physical files in a \"Supported File\" folder, linked to the patient JSON via the `DownloadedFilePath` field in the \"Documents / Images\" array. The page includes a JSON example showing this structure:\n\n```json\n\"Documents / Images\": [\n    {\n        \"FileDescription\": \"Patient's Insurance Card\",\n        \"FolderName\": \"Insurance Card\",\n        \"FileType\": \"jpg\",\n        \"FileCreationDate\": \"2023-09-06T05:21:39.97\",\n        \"DownloadedFilePath\": \"~/Supported File/12345_Front_98765.jpg\"\n    }\n]\n```\n\n### Data Dictionary\n\nThe data dictionary is the primary technical documentation artifact. It is an XLS file with 84 sheets:\n\n- **3 metadata sheets**: Confidential Notice, INDEX (table of contents with category/module classification), Changelogs (7 versions from initial release through Sprint 69/USCDI v3.1, last updated November 2025)\n- **81 data section sheets**, each documenting the fields exported for a specific data domain\n\nEach data section sheet contains:\n- A description of the section\n- A field-level table with columns: Row #, Name of the Column, Data Type, Nullable, Used For (description), Remarks\n\nThe 81 sections cover **960 total fields**. Field data types include String, INT, Date, DateTime, and Boolean. The \"Used For\" column provides brief English descriptions. The \"Remarks\" column is sparsely populated.\n\n### Changelog History\n\n| Version | Date | Description |\n|---------|------|-------------|\n| 1 | 2023-09-13 | Initial Release |\n| 2 | 2023-10-11 | Second release with new sections |\n| 3 | 2023-10-15 | Third release with new sections |\n| 4 | 2024-07-30 | Sprint 36 — new fields added |\n| 5 | 2025-01-24 | Sprint 49 — Fractional Units in Patient Program |\n| 6 | 2025-06-27 | Sprint 60 — Out-of-Pocket Payments in Collection Information |\n| 7 | 2025-10-31 | Sprint 69 — changes related to USCDI v3.1 |\n\nThe dictionary file metadata confirms last save date of 2025-11-17.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe InSync EHI export data dictionary demonstrates **remarkably broad coverage** across clinical and billing domains, which is notable for a (b)(10) compliance effort. Here is a domain-by-domain assessment, referencing the product research:\n\n**Well-covered clinical domains:**\n- **Patient demographics** (64 fields) — comprehensive, including SSN, race, ethnicity, preferred language, communication preferences, emergency contacts\n- **Allergies** (11 fields) — includes RXCUI, SNOMED codes, severity, reactions\n- **Problems/Diagnoses** (8 fields) — ICD codes, onset/resolved dates\n- **Medications** (12 fields) — includes RXCUI, prescribing details, sig instructions\n- **Immunizations** (18 fields) — CVX codes, manufacturer, lot numbers\n- **Vital signs** (43 fields) — very detailed, including BMI, blood pressure, pulse ox, pain scale\n- **Clinical notes** (13 fields) — encounter notes with provider, date, type\n- **Lab results / Clinical tests** (12 fields) — LOINC codes, values, units, reference ranges\n- **Procedures** (10 fields) — CPT/HCPCS codes, modifiers\n- **Goals** (11 fields) — treatment goals with target dates and status\n- **Care team members** (8 fields) — providers, therapists\n- **Referrals** (6 fields) — referral tracking\n- **Family health history** (15 fields) — conditions by family member\n- **Medical devices** (14 fields) — implant details with UDI\n\n**Well-covered billing/financial domains:**\n- **Claims** (18 fields) — claim numbers, payers, submission dates, transmission types\n- **Billing codes assigned** (3 fields) — CPT code linkage\n- **Billing statements and summaries** (6 fields)\n- **Charges, refunds, deductibles** (6 fields) — payment details\n- **Collection information** (3 fields) — copay collection\n- **Denials** (4 fields) — claim denials\n- **Explanation of benefits** (8 fields) — ERA/payer remittance\n- **Health insurance information** (46 fields) — very detailed payer coverage\n- **Eligibility information** (6 fields)\n- **Prior authorizations** (21 fields) — authorization tracking\n- **Price estimates** (17 fields) — patient cost estimates\n- **Financial assistance** (16 fields) — sliding fee scale\n- **List of prices/charges** (2 fields)\n\n**Specialty and behavioral health domains:**\n- **Psychiatric history** (7 fields) — directly relevant to InSync's behavioral health focus\n- **Behavioral data** (4 fields)\n- **Substance abuse** (5 fields) — consumption details\n- **Assessments and treatment plans** (7 fields) — treatment planning data\n- **Social history** (13 fields for consumption, 2 for elements)\n- **Smoking status** (8 fields)\n\n**OB/GYN and specialty clinical data:**\n- **Pregnancy information** (13 fields)\n- **Newborn delivery** (26 fields)\n- **Gynecological history** (18 fields)\n- **Past pregnancy data** (19 fields)\n- **Medical history (Ob/Gyn)** (13 fields)\n- **Pregnancy intent** (2 fields), **Pregnancy status** (5 fields)\n\n**Additional domains:**\n- **Documents/images** (5 fields) — with physical file export\n- **Additional/clinical forms** (4 fields) — custom forms\n- **Custom fields** (2 fields) — demographic custom fields\n- tracking\n- **Recorded sex or gender** (4 fields)\n- **Review of results** (7 fields), **Special alerts for care handoffs** (10 fields)\n- **Event logs** (12 fields) — audit trail (note: this is included in the export even though audit logs are not strictly EHI)\n\n**Potentially missing or underrepresented domains:**\n\nBased on the product research, InSync stores data for several features that are not clearly represented in separate data dictionary sections:\n\n- **ePrescribing / EPCS data**: Medication prescriptions are in the Medications section, but specific ePrescribing transaction details (PDMP queries, controlled substance prescription records, pharmacy transmission status) are not explicitly broken out. Some of this may be captured in the Medications fields.\n- **eMAR (electronic medication administration records)**: Not explicitly called out as a separate section, though it may be included within Medications or Clinical notes.\n- **Telehealth session metadata**: No dedicated section for telehealth session records (video session details, session duration, participant info). These may be captured as encounter subtypes.\n- **Secure messaging**: Patient portal secure messages are not explicitly listed as an exported data domain.\n- **Patient portal activity**: Portal-specific data (login history, form submissions via portal) is not represented.\n- **HL7 interface data**: Only \"HL7 ADT notifications\" (2 fields) is present — this is a narrow view of the interface capabilities.\n\nThese gaps are relatively minor and characteristic of a product that exports from its database rather than cherry-picking USCDI elements. Most of these data types may be subsumed within the broad sections (e.g., telehealth encounters within Encounter information, secure messages within Clinical notes or Documents).\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **vendor-defined flat JSON format** — not FHIR, C-CDA, or any recognized standard. This is appropriate for a (b)(10) export because:\n\n1. The format is clearly designed to export the full breadth of InSync's data model, not just USCDI elements.\n2. The field names are descriptive and self-documenting (e.g., `PatientFirstName`, `ClaimNumber`, `AllergiesRXCUI`).\n3. The data dictionary provides adequate field-level documentation for a developer to parse the export.\n4. Clinical codes (ICD, CPT, LOINC, RXCUI, SNOMED, CVX) are preserved in their standard coded forms.\n\nThe format is NOT standardized interoperability-ready (no FHIR, no C-CDA), which means a receiving system would need to build a custom import. However, this is expected for (b)(10) and is arguably more complete than a FHIR-only export would be.\n\nThere is no JSON Schema, OpenAPI spec, or other machine-readable format specification beyond the XLS data dictionary itself.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- The data dictionary is extensive (81 sections, 960 fields) and covers both EMR and PM modules.\n- Each field has a name, data type, nullable flag, and brief description.\n- The INDEX sheet provides category/module classification for each section.\n- The changelog shows active maintenance (7 versions over 2+ years, most recently November 2025 with USCDI v3.1 updates).\n- The export page clearly separates single-patient vs. population export workflows.\n- The JSON example for document/attachment linking is helpful.\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample export file** — no example JSON showing a complete patient record. Only one JSON snippet for the Documents/Images array.\n- **No JSON schema** — the data dictionary is in XLS format only. No machine-readable schema (JSON Schema, XSD, etc.).\n- **Field descriptions are minimal** — the \"Used For\" column often just restates the column name (e.g., `PatientFirstName` → \"Patient First Name\").\n- **No value sets documented** — coded fields (e.g., `AllergiesSeverityCode`, `PatientGender`, `TransmissionType`) do not specify the allowed values or code systems.\n- **No relationship documentation** — the export appears to be a flat JSON structure per patient, but how the 81 sections relate to each other (e.g., which encounters link to which claims) is not specified.\n- **No cardinality information** — which sections are arrays vs. single values is not documented (though the JSON example implies arrays).\n- **The \"Remarks\" column is almost entirely empty** across all 960 fields.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\nThe field-level documentation is at a **moderate** level of granularity:\n- Field names: yes\n- Data types: yes (String, INT, Date, DateTime, Boolean)\n- Nullable: yes\n- Brief descriptions: yes (though often tautological)\n- Value sets/enumerations: no\n- Relationships/foreign keys: no\n- Cardinality: no\n- Examples: no (except one JSON snippet)\n\nA developer receiving an InSync EHI export could identify and parse the fields but would need to reverse-engineer the structure, relationships, and coded values from actual export data.\n\n### Overall Assessment\n\nThis is a **genuinely strong (b)(10) implementation** that exports across the full breadth of InSync's clinical and billing data model using a vendor-native JSON format. It is clearly not a repackaging of the (g)(10) FHIR API — the export covers 81 distinct data domains including billing, insurance, financial assistance, case management, and specialty-specific clinical data that would not appear in a USCDI/US Core export.\n\nThe primary limitation is **documentation quality rather than export scope**. The data dictionary covers the right breadth of data but lacks the depth (value sets, relationships, examples, machine-readable schema) that would make it fully self-describing. A developer working with this export would need access to sample data to fill in the gaps.\n\nThe export is actively maintained (last updated November 2025 with USCDI v3.1 changes) and has evolved through 7 versions since its September 2023 initial release. The 30-day timeline for population exports and the Terms of Use requirement for population exports are notable operational constraints but not documentation gaps.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://documentation.qualifacts.com/ehi-export/platform/insync/insync-ehi-export.html\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (one click to data dictionary download)\n- Anti-bot issues: Cloudflare present but no challenge served; standard User-Agent header sufficient\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- None.
